<a id="illegal-routes"></a>

# Illegal routes

Sources checked up to 23 Nov 2026.

The irregular routes people from Bangladesh are sold, what happens on them and how they end, and the law and help in Bangladesh, as of November 2026. Each country's own doc covers irregular entry to that country. A raised number such as 3 is an official source; N3 is a news, NGO (non-governmental organisation), research or guide source, listed under "Needs an official source"; a year, like N3 (2022), marks a source older than 2025. "Need to know" marks a point no source has yet confirmed.

<a id="in-one-minute"></a>

## 1 In one minute

Every irregular route sold in Bangladesh ends in one of a few places: a Libyan cell with a phone held to your ear while your family is asked for Tk 25 lakh, a guarded compound in Cambodia or Myanmar where you scam strangers or are beaten, a Russian trench, a Gulf or Maldives street with no permit, a Thai or Malaysian immigration depot, or an airport counter where a fake visa is found and you are sent home <sup>[N1](#n1), [N2](#n2), [N3](#n3), [N4](#n4), [N5](#n5), [N6](#n6), [1 (2024)](#o1), [N7](#n7), [N8](#n8), [N9](#n9)</sup>. The dalal (broker) usually sells it as something legal: a "work visa", a "European work permit", a "free visa", "computer operator" or "construction" work, or "Italy, with some work in Libya first" <sup>[N10](#n10), [N11 (2021)](#n11), [N12](#n12), [N13](#n13), [N14](#n14)</sup>. Common signs: a tourist or visit visa instead of a work visa, a route through Dubai, money split before and after, and a promise that papers will be fixed on arrival <sup>[N15](#n15), [N16](#n16), [N2](#n2), [N17 (2024)](#n17)</sup>. Many victims left with real emigration clearance from BMET (the Bureau of Manpower, Employment and Training) and still ended up in scam compounds <sup>[N18](#n18), [N19](#n19)</sup>; C R Abrar of RMMRU (the Refugee and Migratory Movements Research Unit, a research group) said in July 2024 that workers who came back during Covid had been trafficked although they went abroad with government clearance <sup>[N20 (2024)](#n20)</sup>. Taking the boat to Italy is often the last stage of an attempt at legal migration that failed <sup>[N10](#n10), [N16](#n16)</sup>. Men who went to Libya or Russia for ordinary jobs, some years earlier, have also been kidnapped for ransom or recruited into the war once there (see [Ransom and resale](#ransom-and-resale), and Legal jobs in Russia) <sup>[N21 (2024)](#n21), [N22](#n22), [N23](#n23)</sup>. Shariful Islam Hasan of BRAC said in June 2026 that about a lakh people go abroad legally each month, yet many still try risky routes because the legal process is complicated, expensive and in many cases controlled by broker rings <sup>[N24](#n24)</sup>. A Prothom Alo editorial of 12 January 2024 said a government report found trafficking happens more in areas facing economic crisis and climate-change risk, and that people fall into brokers' hands because going abroad through official channels costs more <sup>[N25 (2024)](#n25)</sup>. Cumilla's additional superintendent of police for the Sadar circle said in April 2026 that people fall into dalals' hands more because they do not know the rules, and that those who go through illegal channels are mostly the ones who face more problems <sup>[N26](#n26)</sup>. Besides the boat from Libya, people have gone from Dubai through Oman, Iran and Turkey to Greece (see [From the Gulf overland through Iran and Turkey to Greece](#from-the-gulf-overland-through-iran-and-turkey-to-greece)), and through South America to the United States' border with Mexico (see [The United States through South America and Mexico](#the-united-states-through-south-america-and-mexico)) <sup>[N27 (2024)](#n27), [N28 (2024)](#n28)</sup>. In Bangladesh, selling a fake job, trading visas, forging papers for smuggling and smuggling people are crimes with prison terms, and a trafficking victim cannot be charged for illegal entry or a false travel document that resulted from the trafficking <sup>[2](#o2), [3](#o3)</sup>. Families can complain to the CID (the police's Criminal Investigation Department), local police, BMET and the Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare, and BRAC and IOM (the International Organization for Migration, the UN migration agency) help with returns (see [Where victims and families get help](#where-victims-and-families-get-help)) <sup>[N29](#n29), [N2](#n2), [4](#o4), [5](#o5), [N30](#n30), [6](#o6)</sup>.

<a id="libya-to-italy-by-boat"></a>

## 2 Libya to Italy by boat

<a id="how-it-is-sold"></a>

### 2.1 How it is sold

The offer is Italy, and the price quoted is usually USD 10,000 (about Tk 12.4 lakh): USD 3,000 before leaving and USD 7,000 after reaching Libya <sup>[N10](#n10), [7](#o7)</sup>. An "all-inclusive" package is about USD 14,000 (about Tk 17.4 lakh) <sup>[N10](#n10), [7](#o7)</sup>. In 2026 the Italian Embassy's migration expert said young Bangladeshis pay traffickers Tk 20 to 30 lakh, and families from Cumilla and Chuadanga paid brokers Tk 18 to 34 lakh <sup>[N31](#n31), [N1](#n1), [N2](#n2)</sup>. A common pitch is half the money before departure and the rest "after reaching Italy" <sup>[N2](#n2)</sup>. Others are told they will travel legally, with temporary work in Libya while they wait for the "next visa"; their passports are taken on arrival <sup>[N10](#n10)</sup>. Some are told they are going to Kuwait or Malaysia, and find out Libya was the destination only when their passports are taken in Dubai or Cairo <sup>[N16](#n16)</sup>. A man left unpaid in Dubai was offered Libya by a facilitator there <sup>[N16](#n16)</sup>. In 2025 two men promised jobs in Greece for Tk 15 lakh each were flown to Libya instead <sup>[N32](#n32)</sup>.

A Prothom Alo editorial of 21 February 2026 said local dalals win trust through social ties and point to relatives' success abroad, that the journey begins with a promise of a valid visa and safe travel and the route is changed midway, and that in many cases people are held in Libya or another country and videos of their torture are sent to the family to extract ransom <sup>[N33](#n33)</sup>. Dr Selim Reza, coordinator of the Centre for Migration Studies at North South University, wrote in Prothom Alo on 9 September 2026 that brokers' networks show flashy YouTube videos of an easy way to Europe and use Facebook, imo (a messaging app) and WhatsApp groups to make the offer, keep contact and pass on travel information, and that a Prothom Alo investigation about four years earlier had found Facebook groups used for sending people to Europe across the Mediterranean <sup>[N34](#n34)</sup>. Citing media reports, the member of parliament Rehana Akter told Parliament on 9 November 2026 that traffickers now trap people not only by posing as brokers but also with AI deepfakes, social media and cryptocurrency <sup>[N35](#n35)</sup>. Police sources said in July 2025 that Robijul Islam of Kushtia, who had lived twelve years in Libya, lured young men from villages in Kushtia, Chuadanga, Jhenaidah and nearby districts to Libya, after which ring members filmed them suffering there and sent the videos to their families to demand large sums <sup>[N36](#n36)</sup>.

**How people were drawn in:**

- **Work in Libya first.** Khurshed Mia (35), a vegetable seller in Bhairab, Kishoreganj, said he first went to Libya on 14 June 2023 for BDT 4 lakh on the understanding that the broker would find him work in Libya until he went on to Italy, and that he got work there as a painter <sup>[N37](#n37)</sup>.
- **A legal visa that failed.** The brother of Abul Bashar Akon (32) of Rajoir, Madaripur, who died with his nephew Titu Howlader (19) in the January 2025 sinking, said a BDT 14 lakh contract had been made a year and a half earlier with a broker from Mahendradi to send the two to Italy on an agricultural visa, and that when that broker could not deliver they were forced to decide on the Libya route and made a new contract through another broker <sup>[N38](#n38)</sup>. He said the broker had told the family the two would reach Italy without any problem, that the family first heard of the nephew's death from the broker, and that the broker could no longer be found; the mother said both had told her by phone that they were fine and the broker would give them a "game" (the boat crossing) very soon <sup>[N38](#n38)</sup>.
- **Told by phone after leaving.** Fatema Akter said her husband Shahin Sikdar (26) left home with the broker on 3 February 2024, phoned some days later to say they would be sent from Libya to Italy illegally by sea, and last spoke to the family on the night of 22 March 2024; she said the broker took BDT 18 lakh from them at various times and that she could not stay in her area because of debt pressure <sup>[N39](#n39)</sup>.
- **A neighbour in Libya.** Azmir Hossain (24) of Mirsarai, Chattogram, said Libya-based Shahadat Hossain kept tempting his mother with a job in Italy paying BDT 50,000 a month, took BDT 1.5 lakh in advance, and had Azmir carry the rest of the money himself; a local union parishad (the lowest tier of local government) member said Shahadat is from the same area <sup>[N40 (2024)](#n40)</sup>.
- **A legal visa, then sold.** According to their families, four young men from Raipur union, Anwara, Chattogram, went to Libya legally in February 2024, paying a broker named Zahirul Islam of Raipur BDT 4 lakh 10 thousand each; after arrival the ring took their passports and papers, saying all had got good jobs at a hospital, and sold the four to another ring, which held them and demanded BDT 10 lakh from each family with videos of their torture <sup>[N41 (2024)](#n41)</sup>.
- **A tourist visa.** Nazmul Hossain (25) of Raiganj, Sirajganj, entered Libya on a tourist visa in mid-2023 through a broker for about BDT 5 lakh; a local teacher said in June 2025, after Nazmul died there, that many local people go to work abroad on tourist visas without understanding and get into trouble, more so in Libya <sup>[N42](#n42)</sup>.
- **Romania, then Italy.** According to PBI (the Police Bureau of Investigation) sources, Syed Abdus Salam (42), a farmer of Khalshi, Bagherpara, Jashore, was told by his neighbour Nawab Ali that he could be sent to Romania, then offered Italy instead on a Tk 4 lakh contract; flown from Dhaka on 22 March 2023 through Dubai and Egypt to Libya, he was held in a house, handed to strangers and held for ransom, and his family paid Tk 15 lakh in stages <sup>[N43 (2024)](#n43)</sup>.
- **A returnee who became a broker.** Villagers in Temaria, Akkelpur, Joypurhat, said in March 2024 that the broker Mizanur Rahman alias Dhalu went to Libya a year and a half earlier and began taking people there while his father and younger brother went around collecting people; Rubel Hossain of Gabtoli, Bogura, sold land and handed BDT 4 lakh to the broker's relatives and left in January 2024 <sup>[N44 (2024)](#n44)</sup>. Rubel was later held and beaten (see [Ransom and resale](#ransom-and-resale)) <sup>[N44 (2024)](#n44)</sup>.
- **Friends already there.** Hridoy Chowdhury, who returned from Libya, told a roundtable at the Prothom Alo office in Dhaka on 18 August 2026 that friends who had gone to Libya told him there would be work there and that later one could go on to Greece or Italy, and that he went with the help of a broker from his own area, Habiganj in Sylhet, whom he trusted because he knew him; the broker is still in Libya <sup>[N45](#n45)</sup>.

<a id="the-contract-price-and-the-final-price"></a>

### 2.2 The contract price and the final price

**Prices quoted:**

- The migration analyst Asif Munir told Deutsche Welle (DW, Germany's public international broadcaster) that each person needs BDT 16 to 20 lakh for this journey and that international as well as local rings are involved, as quoted in a Prothom Alo editorial of 6 February 2025 <sup>[N46](#n46)</sup>.
- In February 2025, according to police and local sources in Madaripur, the ringleaders of the broker ring taking people to Italy through Libya, from Rajoir and from Bhanga in Faridpur, take BDT 18 to 25 lakh from each person under a contract for Europe, and several times more must be paid if the person is caught by Libyan police or held by mafia there <sup>[N38](#n38)</sup>.
- A broker of another Madaripur ring, speaking anonymously in February 2025, said local brokers only collect people and money for Bangladeshi brokers living in Libya, taking BDT 12 lakh to send a person to Libya and BDT 4 lakh more for the "game" (the sea crossing), and that most local brokers in Madaripur now work for Miraj Howlader of Mridhakandi, Shikarmangal union, Kalkini, whom he called a big mafia figure in Libya <sup>[N47](#n47)</sup>.
- To pay the broker Monir Sheikh of Rajoir, Kuddus Bepari (see "Recruited while abroad or just back" in the Dalal scams doc) wrote two bighas of land worth BDT 40 lakh to the broker's wife, Hamida Begum; Monir ran a tailoring shop at Rajoir bazar until about two years earlier, married his three daughters to men living in Libya whom locals say are linked to traffickers and mafias there, has bought land worth at least BDT 5 crore in his wife's name in seven months, and, locals say, makes each contract for BDT 16 lakh <sup>[N47](#n47)</sup>.
- On 18 April 2026 the director of Osmani International Airport, Sylhet, said many of the victims heard at that day's roundtable had spent BDT 10 to 15 lakh, and up to BDT 20 lakh, to go abroad <sup>[N48](#n48)</sup>.
- According to an account by Bangladesh's ambassador to Libya, Major General Habib Ullah, quoted by former foreign affairs adviser Md Touhid Hossain in Prothom Alo on 27 April 2026, among 113 irregular migrants sent home from Libya one had paid brokers and mafia BDT 82 lakh in stages and another BDT 73 lakh, and when asked how many had paid a broker more than BDT 20 lakh, over fifty of the 113 raised their hands <sup>[N49](#n49)</sup>.
- On 18 August 2026 Shariful Islam Hasan, associate director of BRAC's Migration Programme and Youth Platform, said people from eight to ten districts, including Madaripur, Shariatpur, Sylhet, Sunamganj, Habiganj, Kishoreganj and Narsingdi, try to reach Europe across the Mediterranean, that many first go to Libya through Egypt or Dubai and are physically tortured there, and that some pay BDT 25 lakh, some BDT 30 lakh, and some as much as BDT 65 to 67 lakh <sup>[N45](#n45)</sup>.
- A Prothom Alo editorial of 19 August 2026, citing media reports, said people hand dalals Tk 20 to 22 lakh to go to Italy illegally and Tk 12 to 15 lakh to go to Greece <sup>[N50](#n50)</sup>.
- In a BRAC study of 557 returnees from Libya released in February 2024, 56% said they raised the money themselves and 23% had financial help from family <sup>[N51 (2024)](#n51)</sup>.
- A Prothom Alo column of 27 July 2026 said young Bangladeshis drown crossing the Mediterranean on "body contracts" of Tk 20 to 25 lakh, and that of the 8 to 9 lakh young people who go abroad for work each year a large share sell their homestead, borrow at high interest and go through dalals, many of those who go by illegal routes being forced into a life like slavery <sup>[N52](#n52)</sup>.

**Cases.** In each case below, the family paid, or was asked for, more money after the migrant reached Libya <sup>[N53 (2024)](#n53), [N39](#n39), [N37](#n37), [N54](#n54), [N55](#n55), [N56 (2024)](#n56), [N57](#n57), [N58 (2024)](#n58), [N59](#n59), [N60](#n60), [N61](#n61), [N62](#n62), [N63](#n63)</sup>:

- **Spain promised, Libya delivered.** The father of a young man from Jagannathpur, Sunamganj, alleges in a case filed in Sylhet in December 2024 that he made a verbal agreement on 15 August 2018 to send his son to Spain for BDT 15 lakh through a man who is the labour affairs secretary of a union Awami League unit, and that the son was instead sent to Libya, where ring members tortured him and threatened to kill him unless more money was paid <sup>[N53 (2024)](#n53)</sup>.
- **BDT 12 to 20 lakh, then money for release.** In 2022 and 2023, 24 young men from Shariatpur and Madaripur were taken from home to be sent to Italy from Libya (see Held, jailed or missing); the broker ring took BDT 12 lakh to 20 lakh from the 24 families in various ways, some of the men were detained by police in Libya on two occasions and jailed, some were held hostage and tortured, and they were freed from captivity and jail after money was collected from their families <sup>[N39](#n39)</sup>.
- **BDT 4 lakh to Libya, BDT 19 lakh in all.** Khurshed Mia, who went to Libya on 14 June 2023 (see [Libya to Italy by boat](#libya-to-italy-by-boat)), said that while working in Libya he agreed with a broker to be taken by sea to Italy for BDT 9 lakh and paid BDT 6 lakh in two rounds without reaching Italy; he said the whole attempt cost him BDT 19 lakh, plus several lakh for treatment, and that he had to sell the land his house stood on <sup>[N37](#n37)</sup>.
- **BDT 3 lakh agreed, BDT 12 lakh ransom.** According to their families, three men from Noli Joynagar village, Mathbaria, Pirojpur, left for Libya through a broker in July 2023 on a contract of BDT 3 lakh and went first to Dubai, after which contact was lost; some time later they called on imo from Libya to report torture, and the ring demanded BDT 12 lakh ransom per person <sup>[N54](#n54)</sup>. The wife of one said in January 2025 she had sold 15 katha of land to raise the BDT 12 lakh ransom, and the father of another said he had paid BDT 15 lakh since his son left and did not know how much more he would have to pay <sup>[N54](#n54)</sup>.
- **BDT 9.5 lakh agreed, BDT 45 lakh taken.** The family of Dipu Mia of Bhairab, Kishoreganj, contracted with a broker from the same village for BDT 9.5 lakh to reach Italy through Libya, and relatives said in August 2025 that the brokers had so far taken BDT 45 lakh and were demanding BDT 25 lakh more <sup>[N55](#n55)</sup>. According to the family, he reached Libya on 23 September 2023, was detained a few days later and passed to a mafia group, and a month and a half after that they got a call showing a torture video and demanding BDT 11 lakh within one day <sup>[N55](#n55)</sup>.
- **BDT 22 to 24 lakh, then BDT 10 lakh for release.** Prothom Alo reported in July 2024 that the Bhairab broker Rasel Mia took BDT 24 lakh from the father of Faruk Mia of Bhairab and BDT 22 lakh each from Tofayel Ahmed and Sabuj Mia for Italy; the father of Shafiqul Islam of Shibpur, Narsingdi, said Rasel took BDT 23 lakh and that BDT 10 lakh was now being demanded to free his son <sup>[N56 (2024)](#n56)</sup>.
- **BDT 15 lakh, then BDT 30 lakh more.** According to his family, Md Rasel Mia (25) of Dharmandal village, Nasirnagar, Brahmanbaria (not the Bhairab broker of the same name), went to Libya in early 2024 through a broker from his own village for BDT 15 lakh, raised by selling the ancestral homestead and borrowing from relatives; he was sold to a local ring on arrival, which sent videos of his torture to Bangladesh and took a further BDT 30 lakh from the family in rounds <sup>[N57](#n57)</sup>. According to the family, the ring demanded BDT 10 lakh more a few days before his death, and when the family could not pay he was tortured and died in Libya on 21 February 2025; the family learned of the death the next day through an expatriate, and his father, who said he had paid BDT 50 lakh in stages by selling his homestead and farmland, alleged his son was killed with a poisonous injection <sup>[N57](#n57)</sup>.
- **BDT 4 lakh, then 4.5, then 2.5 more.** Prothom Alo reported on 21 September 2024 that a broker ring sent 26 young men from 25 families in Goalnagar union, Nasirnagar, Brahmanbaria, and Ratani village, Austagram, Kishoreganj, to Libya on the promise of Italy, each paying BDT 11 lakh; nine came home after torture and harassment, some of the rest were captive and several were missing <sup>[N58 (2024)](#n58)</sup>. Returnees said each of them gave the broker Farhad Mia BDT 4 lakh before leaving, that he took BDT 4.5 lakh more from each while holding them in a camp in Libya, and that after they were caught on the boat and jailed and he got them out, he again took BDT 2.5 lakh from each family promising to send them to Italy through the "game" <sup>[N58 (2024)](#n58)</sup>.
- **BDT 30 lakh demanded, then BDT 42 lakh.** Md Saiful (39) of Sundarganj, Gaibandha, went to Libya through a Bangladeshi broker about two years before November 2026; the ransom calls to his family from August 2025 are set out under no proof of life (see [Ransom and resale](#ransom-and-resale)) <sup>[N59](#n59)</sup>.
- **BDT 16 lakh, paid in full in Libya, then death.** According to his family, a ring contracted through the dalal Sentu Mia of Baraicha, Belabo, Narsingdi, to take Sohag Mia (28) of Bhairab, who had worked eight years in Qatar, to Italy by sea for BDT 16 lakh, and took him to Libya about seven months before April 2025 after receiving BDT 5 lakh <sup>[N64](#n64)</sup>. The ring held him and extracted the full contract money in instalments, torturing him whenever he raised it; his wife could speak to him only by paying money the dalals demanded <sup>[N64](#n64)</sup>. His brother in Italy said Sohag fell ill after a beating, was not taken to hospital and died in the ring's custody at the end of March 2025, after which none of the dalals answered their phones <sup>[N64](#n64)</sup>.
- **BDT 17 lakh, 12 lakh, then 10 lakh more.** Sohel (36) of Rajoir, Madaripur, a former auto-rickshaw driver, said he spent BDT 39 lakh in stages trying for Italy: BDT 17 lakh and then BDT 12 lakh to the dalal, and BDT 10 lakh more to another dalal named Sumon to try a "game" on his own <sup>[N65](#n65)</sup>.
- **The "body contract".** According to relatives and neighbours, the family of Saidul Bepari of Madaripur paid BDT 20 lakh ransom to free him in Libya and then agreed a BDT 16 lakh "body contract" through a Bangladeshi broker, an arrangement under which brokers promise to deliver the migrant alive to Italy however long it takes and bear lodging, food and airfare; he died in a Libyan detention camp on 14 February 2025 after torture <sup>[N60](#n60)</sup>.
- **BDT 22 lakh, then BDT 20 lakh more.** According to his family, a BDT 22 lakh contract was made to send Imran Khan of Madaripur to Italy, brokers took BDT 20 lakh more while he was held in a detention camp in Benghazi, and he was shot dead by pirates on a migrant boat on 1 November 2025, which the family learned from a broker on 18 November <sup>[N61](#n61)</sup>.
- **BDT 25 lakh, then BDT 22.5 lakh more.** According to relatives and local sources, a broker first took BDT 25 lakh from Tachhir Fakir of Rajoir, Madaripur, for the journey to Italy; brokers in Libya then held him in a detention camp and took BDT 20 lakh ransom from his family and another BDT 2.5 lakh said to be for treatment, and he died in a Libyan hospital in May 2026 <sup>[N62](#n62)</sup>. The family sold all its land to pay <sup>[N62](#n62)</sup>.
- **BDT 60 lakh in three rounds.** Mizanur Rahman of Phulsuti village, Nagarkanda, Faridpur, alleged at a human chain outside the Faridpur Deputy Commissioner's office on 14 October 2026 that a Faridpur trafficking ring, having promised to take him to Italy, held and tortured him in Libya and took BDT 60 lakh in three rounds <sup>[N63](#n63)</sup>.

<a id="the-path"></a>

### 2.3 The path

1. <a id="the-path-item-1"></a>**Dhaka to Dubai** on a tourist visa <sup>[N16](#n16)</sup>. Azmir Hossain said he reached Dubai on 21 March 2024, where the broker's men met him at the airport and kept 30 to 35 people together in a room of a small house for two days <sup>[N40 (2024)](#n40)</sup>.
2. <a id="the-path-item-2"></a>**Dubai to Benghazi**, directly or through Egypt; when checks at Dhaka airport tighten, groups are sent through India and Sri Lanka instead <sup>[N10](#n10), [N16](#n16)</sup>. One 2026 victim was routed Chennai, Sri Lanka, Dubai, Alexandria, Benghazi and Tripoli; another group went through Mumbai, Saudi Arabia and Egypt <sup>[N66](#n66), [N2](#n2)</sup>. According to Palong Model police station and relatives, 24 Shariatpur and Madaripur men taken from home in 2022 and 2023 were sent first to the United Arab Emirates, then through Egypt to Libya <sup>[N39](#n39)</sup>. Azmir Hossain said his group was flown from Dubai to Egypt, held 24 hours in a warehouse-like place in the airport area, and put on another plane to Libya <sup>[N40 (2024)](#n40)</sup>. In BRAC's 2024 study of 557 returnees most had gone Dhaka-Dubai-Egypt-Libya; others went through Istanbul and Dubai, through Qatar, or through Dubai and Syria, and a few flew direct <sup>[N51 (2024)](#n51)</sup>. Mashiur Rahman (38) of Madaripur left in December 2025 through Malaysia (12 hours), Sri Lanka (2 days) and Kuwait (2 hours) to Egypt, waited five months there, and was then taken by a dalal to Al Khums on Libya's coast <sup>[N65](#n65)</sup>. Others go first to Saudi Arabia, some performing Umrah there, then through Kuwait or straight to Egypt and on to Libya <sup>[N67](#n67), [N68](#n68)</sup>.
3. <a id="the-path-item-3"></a>**Across Libya by road**, west through Ajdabiya, Sirte and Shwerayif to Zuwara and Zawiya, the main departure points since 2023 <sup>[N10](#n10)</sup>. Entry to Benghazi is on clearances from the Libyan National Army's Military Investment Authority, the armed force that controls the east <sup>[N10](#n10)</sup>.
4. <a id="the-path-item-4"></a>**The boat to Lampedusa**, Italy's southern island, usually 24 to 36 hours in fair weather <sup>[N10](#n10)</sup>. The crossing itself is known as the "game"; small boats are mainly used and are loaded beyond capacity <sup>[N38](#n38)</sup>. Before the boat people wait in a "game house": Mashiur Rahman was kept about three months in one in Al Khums while his family paid the dalal BDT 32 lakh in stages, handed to the dalal's local representative in Bangladesh <sup>[N65](#n65)</sup>.

**From eastern Libya to Greece.** Boats also leave Tobruk in eastern Libya for Greece, and one sank south of the Greek island of Gavdos in September 2026 <sup>[N69](#n69), [N50](#n50), [N68](#n68)</sup>. What happens on arrival is under "Irregular entry: Libya to Crete by boat" in the Greece doc. How the Greece boat is sold:

- **The price:** Tk 12 to 15 lakh, according to a Prothom Alo editorial of 19 August 2026 <sup>[N50](#n50)</sup>.
- **Split payments:** half the contract money before leaving and the rest when the "game" is ready, a villager of Tarapasha, Dirai, Sunamganj, said in March 2026 <sup>[N70](#n70)</sup>. Shayek Ahmad's father agreed with the dalal Azizul Islam of Ichgaon on Tk 4.5 lakh before leaving and Tk 7.5 lakh after reaching Libya, before the boat <sup>[N67](#n67)</sup>. Families in Adarshapara, Bhairab, agreed Tk 14 lakh each with a local dalal, Sohag Mia (not the Sohag Mia who died in Libya in 2025), in 2026: Tk 9 lakh first and Tk 5 lakh after reaching Greece <sup>[N68](#n68)</sup>.
- **A failed legal attempt first:** two years before Shayek died, his father had sold land and paid a dalal Tk 4.5 lakh to send him to Serbia; it never happened and the money was never recovered <sup>[N67](#n67)</sup>.
- **The broker network:** the Tarapasha men who died in March 2026 went to Libya through a dalal named Jasim Uddin of Dowarabazar, who has local representatives in the villages; local people said the uncle of another of the dead, Jamal Uddin, sends people to Greece via Libya, that his brother Abdur Rahim alias Jasim Uddin is in Libya, and that both face cases in the Sunamganj trafficking tribunal <sup>[N70](#n70)</sup>.

Md Touhid Hossain, the former foreign affairs adviser, wrote in April 2026 that the main route to Italy by sea used to be a flight to Kenya, then overland to Libya, then the boat, that a person boards the plane to Kenya on a false story of a safari or tourism, and that along the way people change hands several times like goods <sup>[N49](#n49)</sup>. As Foreign Adviser in January 2026 he had said that a village youth who goes to Kenya on a tourist visa cannot really afford to go to Kenya or Turkey, and that until this is stopped people will keep dying in the Mediterranean <sup>[N71](#n71)</sup>. He also wrote that because surveillance and restrictions on the route to Italy had recently increased, traffickers were now using the Greece route <sup>[N49](#n49)</sup>. UNHCR (the UN refugee agency) data show that in 2026 Bangladeshis were for the first time the top nationality among sea crossings to Greece, Prothom Alo reported on 30 July 2026 <sup>[N72](#n72)</sup>.

The chain is run in pieces: brokers in Bangladesh recruit and collect money, facilitators in the UAE, Egypt, Kuwait, Turkey, India and Sri Lanka move groups, and Libyan airport sponsors, armed groups, drivers and coastal smugglers control everything inside Libya <sup>[N16](#n16)</sup>. The Counter Terrorism and Transnational Crime (CTTC) unit of the police identified a Libya-based trafficker who had appointed 40 brokers across Bangladesh whose job was to find young people wanting to go abroad, according to a Prothom Alo editorial of 25 February 2024 <sup>[N73 (2024)](#n73)</sup>. According to at least ten families in Kalkini, Madaripur, who spoke to Prothom Alo in September 2025, the Libya-based Miraj Howlader of Mridhakandi, Shikarmangal union, is a member of a trafficking ring in Libya and for 15 years used a local man named Faisal for trafficking and for collecting ransom from the families of local young men held in Libya <sup>[N74](#n74)</sup>. A Prothom Alo investigation published on 3 August 2026 found that a ring including Alauddin Sheikh, whose house stands at Satbaria bazar, Kalia, Narail, took people to Libya promising Italy, held them hostage and sent torture videos to their families to extract ransom, and with the money built houses and bought cars and farms; transactions of Tk 376 crore were found, and a case was filed against 34 people including the alleged leaders Alauddin and Awal <sup>[N75](#n75), [N76](#n76)</sup>.

<a id="ransom-and-resale"></a>

### 2.4 Ransom and resale

- **Ransom.** Smugglers call Bangladeshis "gold" because a family at home will answer ransom calls <sup>[N16](#n16)</sup>. Men are held, tortured and forced to phone home for more money; five Chuadanga men were held 10 months in a room with 57 others <sup>[N2](#n2)</sup>. In July 2026 families of six men from Manoharganj, Cumilla, were asked Tk 25 lakh per person, and in November 2026 the same sum was reportedly being demanded for each of more than 300 men from Bhairab, Kishoreganj <sup>[N1](#n1), [N2](#n2)</sup>. People in the migration sector told Prothom Alo in July 2026 that workers bound for Italy or Greece are sent to Libya, tortured in many camps there, and held hostage until BDT 25 to 30 lakh is extracted <sup>[N72](#n72)</sup>. Bangladeshi and Libyan smugglers stage kidnappings together <sup>[N10](#n10)</sup>. Shariful Islam of BRAC told Prothom Alo in August 2026 that Libya has more than a hundred camps where people are held hostage and tortured, and that ransoms of Tk 5 lakh to Tk 76 lakh have been taken from families <sup>[N77](#n77)</sup>. Shayek Ahmad's father said the dalals called it a camp, but everyone was crammed into one room, many had their phones taken, they were beaten for money and photos and videos of the beatings were sent home so the money would come faster; when his son begged to be brought home, the dalal said time was up and the "game" could come at any moment <sup>[N67](#n67)</sup>. Md Touhid Hossain wrote in April 2026 that many Bengalis are among those who torture the hostages in Libya, and that the people who collect ransom from families in Bangladesh by showing torture videos live undisturbed in the same society <sup>[N49](#n49)</sup>.
- **Kidnapped after years of work.** Men who went to Libya for ordinary jobs are also taken:
  - Four masons in Benghazi from Lalmonirhat and Kurigram, one of whom had been sent three years earlier for BDT 5 lakh raised by selling land, were kidnapped in March 2024; one family was first asked for BDT 1 lakh, sent as BDT 50,000 to each of two bKash numbers, another was asked BDT 5 lakh per person and told the men would be killed if the families went to the law <sup>[N21 (2024)](#n21)</sup>.
  - Four men from Charpara, Gurudaspur, Natore, who had gone two years earlier for BDT 4 lakh each (from mortgaged land, sold cows and loans) and had sent home about BDT 2 lakh per family on average, were tortured on video on 2 June 2024 with a demand for BDT 10 lakh each <sup>[N78 (2024)](#n78)</sup>.
  - Abdur Rob of Noakhali, sent on 9 January 2022 for BDT 10 lakh, worked with his brother at a medicine maker; on 14 January 2025 men identifying themselves as law enforcement stopped four of them on the way to Misrata, released three after checking papers and kept him, and on 26 January a caller told the family they had "bought" him for BDT 20 lakh and would free him for BDT 26 lakh <sup>[N22](#n22)</sup>.
  - Golam Rabbani of Akkelpur, Joypurhat, in Libya since 2015, was kidnapped in Zliten after being called for painting work, by what his wife called a local Bengali mafia ring; she was called from his phone on 30 November 2025 with a demand for BDT 30 lakh, later BDT 15 lakh; Abdul Karim, in Libya 13 years, was called out on 6 December 2025 "to look at painting work" and held for the same BDT 30 lakh, and one family managed to send BDT 1 lakh <sup>[N79](#n79)</sup>.
  - According to RAB (the Rapid Action Battalion, a police unit), Sumon Mia of Sonargaon, in Libya eight years, gave his expired passport to a man named Abu Bakkar to renew in June 2025; on 13 September he phoned to say Abu Bakkar and three or four others had kidnapped him, the family sent BDT 3 lakh 17 thousand to three bKash numbers and then lost contact, and on 20 December a caller asked for BDT 3 to 4 lakh more <sup>[N80](#n80)</sup>.
- **Where the ransom is paid.** Families are told to pay in Bangladesh, not Libya: to bKash numbers of the ring in Bangladesh, in stages <sup>[N21 (2024)](#n21), [N80](#n80)</sup>; into five Bangladeshi bank accounts, which Abdur Rob's father found had been opened in Chattogram and Khulna <sup>[N22](#n22)</sup>; or into an account at the Motijheel corporate branch of Dutch-Bangla Bank <sup>[N79](#n79)</sup>.
- **The kidnappers speak Bangla.** Families of the Anwara men said the ring spoke to them in Bangla and called every hour for the ransom; Rubel Hossain's sister-in-law got the BDT 10 lakh demand from an unknown Bangladeshi voice; the Natore men told their families the kidnappers were Bangladeshi <sup>[N41 (2024)](#n41), [N44 (2024)](#n44), [N78 (2024)](#n78)</sup>.
- **No proof of life.** According to investigators, on 5 August 2025 the wife of Md Saiful of Sundarganj, Gaibandha, got a call from a Libyan mobile number saying he was in the callers' custody, that BDT 30 lakh ransom must be paid or he would be killed, and that no one in the family would be allowed to speak to him until half the ransom was paid <sup>[N59](#n59)</sup>. The CID said the family sent BDT 5 lakh in all between 10 and 25 August 2025 from a bKash (mobile money) shop at Bamandanga Hall Mor, Sundarganj, to several mobile numbers the accused gave, and that even after the money was received the family was not put in touch with Saiful <sup>[N59](#n59)</sup>. The father was then contacted from another number and asked for a further BDT 42 lakh in the name of rescuing his son, and was told his son was held by two men in Libya named Mokarram and Anwar from whom the caller had got the father's number; the father said he would pay nothing more unless he could speak to his son directly, and no more calls came to him or to his son's wife <sup>[N59](#n59)</sup>.
- **No one guarantees release.** Families of two hostages in the Bhairab case said in July 2024 that large ransoms were being demanded but that they could find no one who could assure them that paying would get the men released <sup>[N56 (2024)](#n56)</sup>. According to local people in Bhairab, when the hostages phoned home begging to be freed, the broker Rasel Mia and his ring members hid and cut off contact with the families; the families then went to community leaders for a remedy and got no result <sup>[N56 (2024)](#n56)</sup>.
- **Families stay silent.** The wife of one Mathbaria hostage refused to name the brokers to Prothom Alo in January 2025, saying she wanted her husband back, and the family of another refused to speak at all, believing someone known to the brokers lived near their home and that the torture would increase if word got out <sup>[N54](#n54)</sup>. The officer-in-charge of Gurudaspur police station said in June 2024 that the Natore families, advised to complain, were afraid to because of the kidnappers <sup>[N78 (2024)](#n78)</sup>. The wife of one kidnapped Lalmonirhat worker said her in-laws told her not to come back to her husband's home unless he was freed <sup>[N21 (2024)](#n21)</sup>.
- **Sold on arrival, and one escape.** The four Anwara men were sold to a second ring soon after they landed; one, Wasim (20), escaped by breaking the ventilator of the toilet of the room they were held in and told local law enforcement, who raided the place, but the traffickers had already moved the other three <sup>[N41 (2024)](#n41)</sup>.
- **Sold between gangs.** Seven Bangladeshis were sold for USD 17,000 together, and the buyer demanded USD 12,000 each <sup>[N16](#n16)</sup>. Azmir Hossain said everyone's passport was taken as soon as they reached Libya in March 2024, that he was beaten whenever he mentioned the promised job, and that BDT 6 lakh was collected from his family in stages by phone <sup>[N40 (2024)](#n40)</sup>. He said the broker then sold him to another group at a high price and left him in a Libyan desert to be handed over; he escaped with a Libyan citizen's help and took shelter with a Bangladeshi he knew, and the broker then came and took him back <sup>[N40 (2024)](#n40)</sup>. Suman Mia of Nasirnagar said in September 2024 that he paid the broker BDT 11 lakh in all and was told he would be taken to Italy within a week; 60 people were put on a boat from Benghazi, and after it had run for an hour they understood they had all been sold <sup>[N58 (2024)](#n58)</sup>. Enamul Haque of Lalurtuk village, Nasirnagar, said he spent BDT 22 lakh to send two sons (land sold for BDT 7 lakh, a BDT 12 lakh loan and savings), that one son was "sold four times", and that he last spoke to the other a year earlier; both were missing in September 2024 <sup>[N58 (2024)](#n58)</sup>.
- **Extra charges.** Charges added in Libya push the total to USD 15,000 to 17,000 (about Tk 18.7 to 21.2 lakh) <sup>[N10](#n10), [7](#o7)</sup>. In the last holding houses before the sea ("game houses") smugglers demand more even from people told the trip was fully paid <sup>[N10](#n10)</sup>. Those told they can pay after reaching Europe are made to work in Libya to pay off the debt <sup>[N10](#n10)</sup>.
- **Paying does not free you.** In a 2025 case the families paid Tk 21.8 lakh and Tk 16 lakh in ransom, and the men were then handed to Libyan police and held 45 days <sup>[N32](#n32)</sup>. People caught at sea by the Libyan Coast Guard report being handed to Bangladeshi networks on the coast who demand more <sup>[N10](#n10)</sup>.
- **Killed when the family could not pay.** At the Faridpur human chain on 14 October 2026, Taslima Begum said her husband Habib was killed because the family could not pay a ransom of BDT 10 lakh; she now lives with their three children at her father's house <sup>[N63](#n63)</sup>.

<a id="held-jailed-or-missing"></a>

### 2.5 Held, jailed or missing

- **How common it is.** In BRAC's study of 557 returnees, released on 23 February 2024, 63% of those taken to Libya on the promise of Europe had been held captive and most tortured; 68% lost freedom of movement on arrival, 54% never got three meals a day and at least 22% got one; of the captives 93% were held in camps and 79% physically tortured <sup>[N51 (2024)](#n51)</sup>. Local dalals had lured 60% of the families with good jobs, and 89% got no job or work at all <sup>[N51 (2024)](#n51)</sup>.
- **In the camps.** Suman Mia said the 60 sold off the Benghazi boat were held in a camp for a month and a half, beaten every day and fed once in 24 hours, given two litres of salt water for bathing and the toilet and one bread at night, and made to do cleaning work from morning to evening at the house of a man named Ahammad, whose son beat them with a pistol when they did not work <sup>[N58 (2024)](#n58)</sup>. Azmir Hossain said he was locked in a room and beaten regularly for trying to escape and went eight days without food; he reached home on 5 May 2024 in a critical condition, 41 days after leaving home according to Prothom Alo, and his mother said he is now mentally ill from blows to the head and that she owes BDT 6 lakh <sup>[N40 (2024)](#n40)</sup>. Returnees told the ambassador to Libya before their flight home in spring 2026 that one had eaten rice once after twenty days, five men sharing one bowl with no curry, that another got a handful after twenty-five days, and that a piece of bread was thrown at them "the way food is given to a dog" and they were beaten as well <sup>[N49](#n49)</sup>.
- **Raids and jail.** Khurshed Mia said law-enforcement officers raided the fourth-floor flat where 11 of them lived at about 1:30 am on 10 July 2024 and he hid for two hours on the window ledge; later he was kept in a place with 210 other people, where a raid at 10 pm took away 160 while he hid for four hours in a tree <sup>[N37](#n37)</sup>. Returnees from Nasirnagar and Austagram said that two and a half to three months after the last payment the broker sold them, that after three months in jail they reached their families through a Pakistani man with the help of a man from Bhairab, and could come home only after each paid a further BDT 1 lakh 20 thousand for the air ticket and other costs; they returned between 7 August and 14 September 2024 after release from Libyan jail <sup>[N58 (2024)](#n58)</sup>. Hridoy Chowdhury said that after reaching Libya he worked for a company for two months in a row, was then taken to Benghazi and boarded a boat for Greece, and that after about 14 hours on the boat the coast guard detained them and sent them back to Libya, where he was held as a prisoner in a jail in Tobruk <sup>[N45](#n45)</sup>. He said he finally got out of the jail by contacting his broker, and that he had to come home empty-handed despite spending a huge amount of money <sup>[N45](#n45)</sup>.
- **Tajoura detention centre.** Mashiur Rahman said his wooden boat of 30 Bangladeshis and 4 Egyptians from Al Khums was caught by the Libyan army after half an hour and he was held 45 days at Tajoura detention centre in Tripoli <sup>[N65](#n65)</sup>. He said 500 people were kept in a room with space for 200, and the Nigerians in charge beat people daily, even over small things, because they could not understand them <sup>[N65](#n65)</sup>. Breakfast at 10 am was one khubz (thick bread), lunch at 3 pm was pasta, one medium bowl for seven people, the same food came again at night, and they survived on salty tap water <sup>[N65](#n65)</sup>. Sohel, caught an hour or so after his fibre boat left Al Khums, said there was an attempt to sell the group to another trafficker, from whom release would have cost BDT 10 to 12 lakh, so they begged the soldiers to send them to Tajoura instead, because IOM visits it and a way home can open from there <sup>[N65](#n65)</sup>.
- **Escape.** Mizanur Rahman said that, still not released after the payments and seeing how severe the torture was, he broke a window, escaped and gave himself up to Libyan police, and later came home in 2026 through IOM; eleven people tortured by the same ring came home from Libya through IOM in 2026 and joined the human chain in Faridpur <sup>[N63](#n63)</sup>.
- **Missing.** Prothom Alo reported on 13 July 2024 that at least 24 people from Bhairab and elsewhere in Kishoreganj, Habiganj and Narsingdi went to Libya at about the same time through a broker ring led by Rasel Mia of Bhairab to cross to Italy by sea, that none had reached Italy, and that some were hostages of kidnappers and others were in Libyan jails <sup>[N56 (2024)](#n56)</sup>. According to relatives and police, 24 young men from Shariatpur and Madaripur who were taken from home in 2022 and 2023 went missing in Libya; relatives claim none of the 24 has contacted family since 21 and 22 March 2024 <sup>[N39](#n39)</sup>. Riaz Akon, one of their relatives, said his family gave the broker BDT 20 lakh, raised by selling land and a cattle farm, to send his younger brother to Italy, had had no trace of him for a year, and that the broker gave no information when asked <sup>[N39](#n39)</sup>.

<a id="work-in-libya"></a>

### 2.6 Work in Libya

Khurshed Mia said that in Libya, after a month without work, he got a job at a shopping mall "in exchange for 500 dinars" that was to pay 1,400 dinars at the end of the month but paid him half, and then worked four months at an oil company for 1,200 dinars a month <sup>[N37](#n37)</sup>. He said a mafia group attacked the building the broker had moved him to and he jumped from the first floor to save himself, injuring his arm and leg; he got no treatment for five days, the broker's men moved him from a private hospital to a government hospital because they could not bear the cost, he had five operations in three and a half months there, and he came home on 13 February 2025 unable to walk <sup>[N37](#n37)</sup>. Nazmul Hossain, who had entered on a tourist visa in 2023, was burned in a gas cylinder explosion while cooking and died in a Libyan hospital in June 2025 <sup>[N42](#n42)</sup>.

<a id="deaths"></a>

### 2.7 Deaths

- **May 2020, Mizda.** According to Libya's then government, a trafficker's family killed 26 Bangladeshis and four Africans in Mizda, Libya <sup>[N81 (2020)](#n81)</sup>.
- **February 2024, off Tunisia.** On 15 February 2024 a boat carrying 52 migrants caught fire and sank in the Mediterranean off the Tunisian coast; 8 Bangladeshis died, 26 others were rescued and one was seriously injured, according to a Prothom Alo editorial of 25 February 2024 <sup>[N73 (2024)](#n73)</sup>. The Bangladesh embassy in Libya confirmed the eight Bangladeshi dead; the ninth was a Pakistani <sup>[N82 (2024)](#n82)</sup>. Tunisia's navy rescued the 26 Bangladeshis, one of them in critical condition, and the embassy said it was in contact with the Tunisian authorities, the Red Crescent and IOM for their rescue and treatment <sup>[N82 (2024)](#n82)</sup>. According to relatives, police and local sources, several young men from Rajoir, Madaripur, and Muksudpur, Gopalganj, left home for Italy on 14 January 2024, flew through Dubai to Libya, and on 14 February left Libya on an engine boat through brokers; eight of them died on the boat off Tunisia (five from Rajoir and three from Muksudpur), along with one Pakistani <sup>[N83 (2024)](#n83)</sup>. Relatives of the eight told journalists at Dhaka airport on 2 May 2024 that brokers had put 52 people on a small boat that could take 30, that the eight who died had been forced to stay below the deck, and that when they tried to come out for lack of oxygen the brokers beat them and sent them back down; the relatives said they died of the torture and lack of oxygen <sup>[N83 (2024)](#n83)</sup>. The brother of one of the dead, Sajal Bairagi (22) of Sendia village, Rajoir, said the family gave BDT 14 lakh to a broker named Mosharraf by selling land and cows and borrowing <sup>[N83 (2024)](#n83)</sup>.
- **December 2024, off Zawiya.** The Foreign Ministry spokesperson said on 24 December 2024 that a boat carrying 121 migrants was wrecked off the Libyan coast on 18 December; Libya's navy rescued 82 alive and recovered at least 15 bodies, a local Red Crescent volunteer confirmed 8 of the dead and 32 of the rescued were Bangladeshi, the survivors were kept in Zawiya, 80 km west of Tripoli, and embassy officials were trying to meet them with permission from Libya's Foreign Ministry <sup>[N84 (2024)](#n84)</sup>.
- **January 2025, off Libya.** According to the Bangladesh embassy in Libya, a boat carrying 56 people trying to reach Italy from Libya sank in the Mediterranean on 25 January 2025 and 23 bodies washed up on the beach between 28 and 31 January; the bodies were decomposed and not formally identified, but the embassy, citing local sources, said nearly all were Bangladeshi <sup>[N38](#n38)</sup>. No nationality papers were found with the bodies, and being unidentified they were buried at Ajdabiya, 40 km from Brega; two people rescued alive in critical condition were in a hospital controlled by Benghazi militias, and the fate of the other 31 aboard was unknown in February 2025 <sup>[N85](#n85), [N86](#n86)</sup>. The embassy had asked for permission to go to Brega and not yet got it <sup>[N86](#n86)</sup>. According to relatives, police and local sources, two Rajoir men left home on 1 January 2025, flew from Dhaka through Dubai to Libya, were sent by the broker to the coast, and left the Libyan coast on an engine boat on 24 January <sup>[N38](#n38)</sup>. Intelligence sources said in February 2025 that, besides the two dead, eight more people from Rajoir upazila, all aged 20 to 30, were reported missing, though it was not confirmed whether they were on the boat that sank on 25 January <sup>[N38](#n38)</sup>. Relatives said at least 12 people from Madaripur and Faridpur were among those killed, according to a Prothom Alo editorial of 6 February 2025 <sup>[N46](#n46)</sup>. The same editorial reported that the father of Hridoy Howlader of Kamarkhali, Faridpur, said the group was to be taken to Italy by ship on the night of 24 January, that many refused to board when a small boat was offered instead, and that those who refused were shot dead while those who boarded drowned <sup>[N46](#n46)</sup>. It also reported that Sujan Faraji of Madaripur, who died in the sinking, set out with BDT 16 lakh taken from his aunt without telling his father, who had not agreed to the journey, and that a broker named Monir Sheikh of Majumdarkandi, Rajoir, had contracted to take him to Italy for BDT 15 lakh <sup>[N46](#n46)</sup>. Abul Bashar Akon and his nephew Titu Howlader also died (see [Libya to Italy by boat](#libya-to-italy-by-boat)) <sup>[N38](#n38)</sup>. Kuddus Bepari, sent from Dhaka to Egypt in early November 2024 and on to Libya, and his brother-in-law Sujon Howlader (39), who also paid the broker Monir BDT 16 lakh, were put on an engine boat from the Benghazi coast on 24 January 2025 after about two months in a detention house; the boat sank within hours, Sujon's death was confirmed by his wife Munni Begum, and Kuddus was missing, believed dead by his family <sup>[N47](#n47)</sup>. Madaripur's Additional Superintendent of Police (Administration and Finance), Bhaskar Saha, said in February 2025 that by family accounts three people from Rajoir upazila had died and seven were missing, presumed dead, after the recent sinking, that no family had yet come to file a case, and that police were hunting several named brokers <sup>[N47](#n47)</sup>.
- **November 2025, off Al Khums.** A boat with 26 Bangladeshis capsized off Al Khums and four died <sup>[N87](#n87)</sup>.
- **January 2026, central Mediterranean.** IOM said in January 2026 that several boats were feared sunk in the previous 10 days, with several hundred people feared missing or dead <sup>[N88](#n88)</sup>. A boat from Sfax, Tunisia, reached Lampedusa with three dead, two of them twin girls about a year old; a survivor of a second boat that left at the same time from the same place said at least 50 may have died or gone missing <sup>[N88](#n88)</sup>. Most boats leave from Libya or Tunisia, carrying people from Bangladesh, Syria, Afghanistan, Pakistan and African countries <sup>[N88](#n88)</sup>.
- **March 2026, Libya to Greece.** At least 18 Bangladeshis died on a boat that left Tobruk, Libya, for Greece on 21 March <sup>[N69](#n69)</sup>; one survivor said in the first reports that 43 people were aboard, 38 of them Bangladeshi <sup>[N69](#n69)</sup>, a count the later figures (22 dead, 26 rescued, in "Irregular entry: Libya to Crete by boat" in the Greece doc) exceed. Prothom Alo reported on 5 April 2026 that the boat lost its way, was at sea six days and ran out of fuel, and that 22 people on it died for lack of food and water, 18 of them Bangladeshi; 13 were from Sunamganj (six from Dirai upazila, five from Jagannathpur, one from Dowarabazar and one from Chhatak) <sup>[N89](#n89)</sup>. On 5 April three Sunamganj members of parliament visited the families on the Prime Minister's behalf and gave them some gifts <sup>[N89](#n89)</sup>. Md Touhid Hossain wrote in Prothom Alo on 27 April 2026 that a boat that left Libya for Greece on 21 March 2026 with 48 passengers drifted for six days before 26 people were rescued alive, 21 of them Bangladeshis <sup>[N49](#n49)</sup>. A Prothom Alo editorial of 1 April 2026 said the family of Shayek Ahmed (23) of Sunamganj gave brokers BDT 12 lakh by selling their cows and land, that the rubber boat he left on from Libya for Greece on 21 March lost its way and drifted for six days, that he died of starvation when the food ran out, and that the bodies of the young men from Sunamganj who died were thrown into the sea <sup>[N90](#n90)</sup>. According to a survivor, the boat carrying 38 people from Libya lost its way and drifted six days; 18 died for lack of food and clean water, their bodies were kept on the boat two days and then thrown into the sea, and the survivors were taken to a camp after the boat reached the Greek coast on 27 March 2026 <sup>[N91](#n91)</sup>. The rescue and what happened in Greece are under "Deaths on the crossing" in the Greece doc.
- **April 2026, from Tajoura.** On 4 April 2026 a wooden boat that left Tajoura, Libya, for the Italian island of Lampedusa with about 105 passengers sank; 32 people were rescued alive and 2 bodies recovered, the other 71 were missing, and Bangladeshis were among both the rescued and the missing, Md Touhid Hossain wrote <sup>[N49](#n49)</sup>.
- **July 2026, Madaripur.** Prothom Alo reported on 31 July 2026 that a young man from Madaripur died from brokers' torture in Libya on the way to Italy, and that his family alleges he was killed even after BDT 60 lakh was taken <sup>[N92](#n92)</sup>.
- **August 2026, adrift.** A rubber boat built for 25 left Tobruk for Greece on 3 August 2026 with 42 aboard, 38 Bangladeshis and 4 Sudanese; it ran on a speedboat engine with no navigation system, and the passengers had been told the crossing would take about 36 hours, according to survivor Abdul Karim as reported by the Bangladesh Embassy in Cairo <sup>[N50](#n50), [N93](#n93)</sup>. The fuel ran out and the boat drifted about 10 days before reaching the coast at Marsa Matruh, Egypt, on 13 August <sup>[N93](#n93)</sup>. Prothom Alo reported on 17 August 2026 that 29 Bangladeshis were rescued after drifting 10 days, and that 10 people, 9 of them Bangladeshi, died for lack of food and water; the Bangladesh Embassy in Cairo confirmed the deaths, and the 29 were treated in a hospital in Egypt <sup>[N94](#n94), [N50](#n50)</sup>. Abdul Karim said the 10 died of the heat, the sun and lack of food and water, and that the survivors were forced to put the bodies into the sea once they began to decompose <sup>[N93](#n93)</sup>. Local police rescued the 29 when the boat came ashore; 18 were seriously ill and unconscious, and all were admitted to Marsa Matruh General Hospital <sup>[N93](#n93)</sup>. The embassy said they would be sent home once they recovered, after the formal process under Egyptian law <sup>[N93](#n93)</sup>. Prothom Alo reported on 19 August 2026 that seven young men from Sunamganj had gone missing trying to cross the Mediterranean via Libya <sup>[N95](#n95)</sup>.
- **September 2026, off Gavdos.** A boat that left Libya for Greece on the night of 14 September 2026 sank south of the Greek island of Gavdos; the news agency AFP (Agence France-Presse) reported 77 aboard, 51 rescued, one body found and 25 missing <sup>[N68](#n68)</sup>. Of three young men from Adarshapara, Bhairab, two were alive in the custody of Greek law enforcement and Bayezid Mia (20) was still missing on 22 September <sup>[N68](#n68)</sup>.
- **October 2026, Libya to Greece.** According to families and local sources, seven young men from Madhabpur and Lakhai, Habiganj, left Libya for Greece through dalals on 31 October 2026 on an inflatable rubber boat with about fifty passengers; it sprang a leak mid-sea and sank <sup>[N96](#n96)</sup>. Only Masum Mia of Puraikala, Madhabpur, survived, reaching Greece after two days adrift; the other six were still missing on 18 November <sup>[N96](#n96)</sup>. The families said the ring took Tk 12 to 15 lakh from each, raised by selling land or borrowing at high interest, and that they could not reach the dalals, who were in Dhaka <sup>[N96](#n96)</sup>.
- **At sea.** The State Minister for Expatriates' Welfare alleged that smugglers abandon weak or sick passengers at sea and often throw bodies into the water <sup>[N69](#n69)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How many from Rajoir died or went missing in the January 2025 sinking: two dead and eight more missing (intelligence sources), or three dead and seven missing (police, from family accounts) <sup>[N38](#n38), [N47](#n47)</sup>?

<a id="arrival-in-italy"></a>

### 2.8 Arrival in Italy

People landing are taken to a hotspot (a first-reception centre) for checks, photos and fingerprints <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. Italy lists Bangladesh as a safe country of origin and rejected 88.66% of Bangladeshi asylum claims at first decision in 2025 <sup>[9](#o9), [N97](#n97)</sup>. Italy returned over 120 Bangladeshis in 2025 up to early November, including four on 31 October, two of whom had arrived from Libya without a visa a month before <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>.

<a id="coming-home"></a>

### 2.9 Coming home

Men who went this way and failed have come home with debts of more than Tk 10 lakh <sup>[N16](#n16)</sup>. Five Chuadanga families lost about Tk 1.47 crore in fees and ransom <sup>[N2](#n2)</sup>. Returnees flown home on Embassy outpasses (travel permits) are questioned at Dhaka airport and their details taken on a Home Ministry form; on 21 August 2025, among 175 who landed on a charter flight from Tripoli, a man accused in a trafficking case was identified this way on NSI (National Security Intelligence) information and arrested by the CID <sup>[N98](#n98)</sup>. After the 1 September 2026 flight the CID spoke to returnees and gave them contact numbers for legal help to act against their dalals, and IOM asked them to stay in touch about any problem <sup>[N65](#n65)</sup>. Mashiur Rahman of Madaripur, who came home on 1 September 2026 with a group of Bangladeshis returned from detention in Libya with IOM's help, said he had left his own restaurant business to try for Italy and came back having lost BDT 32 lakh <sup>[N99](#n99)</sup>. Bodies can take months: Tarikul Islam (25) of Khetlal, Joypurhat, who went to Libya about seven years earlier, illegally according to a union parishad member, died of illness there on 3 January 2026, and his body reached Dhaka airport on 12 September 2026, late because of legal and administrative complications, the member said <sup>[N100](#n100)</sup>.

<a id="the-numbers"></a>

### 2.10 The numbers

- **Arrivals in Italy by sea:** 20,164 Bangladeshis in 2025 and 6,160 from 1 January to 6 November 2026, more than any other nationality both years <sup>[11](#o11), [12](#o12)</sup>. BRAC, citing Frontex (the EU border agency), said 20,259 Bangladeshis entered Europe across the Mediterranean in 2025, and that in the first six months of 2026, 4,249 Bangladeshis entered Italy this way and 3,608 went from Libya to Greece by sea <sup>[N72](#n72)</sup>. Prothom Alo reported on 5 September 2026 that 66,316 people of all nationalities crossed the Mediterranean to Italy in 2025 and 1,126 of them died or went missing at sea, and that of 14,194 who crossed up to June 2026 the largest share, 32% or 3,691 people, were Bangladeshis <sup>[N99](#n99)</sup>. According to CID information cited in a Prothom Alo editorial of 1 April 2026, 17,000 Bangladeshis were sent to Italy by illegal routes in the previous year <sup>[N90](#n90)</sup>. According to UNHCR, as cited in a Prothom Alo editorial of 25 February 2024, 13 per cent of the 5,236 refugees and migrant workers who went to Italy by sea were Bangladeshi citizens; the editorial does not state the period <sup>[N73 (2024)](#n73)</sup>.
- **Share of the route:** Bangladeshis were 50% of people leaving Libya in December 2024 and 39% in December 2025; 88% of all arrivals in Italy in 2025 left from Libya <sup>[N101](#n101), [N102](#n102)</sup>. Frontex counted 2,510 Bangladeshis among 8,489 crossings on the Central Mediterranean route in January to April 2026 <sup>[N16](#n16)</sup>. Bangladeshis were again in 2026 the top nationality among people crossing the Mediterranean to Italy, according to UNHCR data reported by Prothom Alo on 30 July 2026 <sup>[N72](#n72)</sup>.
- **Over the years:** 4,141 Bangladeshis arrived by this route in 2020, 7,838 in 2021 and 15,228 in 2022; Frontex counts about 110,000 from 2009 to June 2026 <sup>[N16](#n16), [N2](#n2)</sup>. A Prothom Alo editorial of 6 February 2025 stated, giving no source, that on average at least 100 Bangladeshis die each year when boats sink <sup>[N46](#n46)</sup>. Shariful Islam Hasan of BRAC said on 18 April 2026 that Bangladeshis are the largest group in the world crossing the Mediterranean to Europe, a situation that has lasted a decade <sup>[N48](#n48)</sup>.
- **All irregular routes to Europe:** researchers of North South University's Centre for Migration Studies wrote in May 2024 that more than 40,000 Bangladeshis went to Europe irregularly in 2023, more than 15,000 of them into Italy <sup>[N103 (2024)](#n103)</sup>. IOM counted 24,318 Bangladeshis reaching Europe by irregular sea and land routes in 2025, up 59% on 2024 <sup>[N104](#n104)</sup>. IOM said in April 2026 that the flow of migrants from Bangladesh had grown and that Bangladeshis had emerged as the largest group of migrants heading to Europe <sup>[N105](#n105)</sup>. The general secretary of the Sylhet District Press Club said on 18 April 2026, giving no source, that over the previous 10 years at least 10,000 people had died or gone missing, more than two and a half lakh had been trafficked abroad by illegal routes and at least 15,000 had been jailed in various countries <sup>[N48](#n48)</sup>. A Prothom Alo editorial of 31 August 2024 said, without a source, that several lakh Bangladeshis are held in prisons abroad for lack of valid papers <sup>[N106 (2024)](#n106)</sup>. Shariful Islam Hasan of BRAC said at a roundtable in Cox's Bazar on 6 June 2026 that about one lakh Bangladeshis have been detained while trying to cross the Mediterranean in the past 16 years, and that about six and a half lakh Bangladeshis currently have asylum applications lodged in various countries <sup>[N24](#n24)</sup>.
- **Clearance:** Prothom Alo reported on 5 September 2026 that only 100 people took BMET clearance to go to Libya in 2025 and 63 in 2026, which it reads as meaning the workers reach Libya through the Middle East and other countries <sup>[N99](#n99)</sup>.
- **Where people come from:** Shariful Islam Hasan of BRAC said on 18 April 2026 that people from 8 to 10 districts, among them Madaripur, Shariatpur, Sylhet, Sunamganj and Habiganj, are losing their lives going this way <sup>[N48](#n48)</sup>. By the ambassador to Libya's account, about two-thirds of 113 returnees on one spring 2026 flight identified themselves as from Madaripur <sup>[N49](#n49)</sup>. BRAC's 2024 study of 557 returnees found people aged 26 to 40, most of all 31 to 35, try most, mostly from Madaripur, Shariatpur, Faridpur, Sylhet, Sunamganj, Munshiganj, Noakhali, Brahmanbaria and Cumilla <sup>[N51 (2024)](#n51)</sup>.
- **Deaths:** 1,718 people died or went missing on the Central Mediterranean in 2024, one for every 39 arrivals, and about 765 by 7 April 2026, over 460 more than a year earlier <sup>[N101](#n101), [N107](#n107)</sup>. A Prothom Alo editorial of 21 February 2026 says, citing UNHCR data, that Bangladeshis were the largest group among migrants who crossed the Mediterranean to Italy from January to October 2024, as in 2023, and states, without naming a source, that more than 25,000 people drowned in the Mediterranean in the past decade, of whom at least 500 each year were Bangladeshi <sup>[N33](#n33)</sup>. Worldwide, IOM said in April 2026 that 7,904 people were reported dead or missing on migration routes in 2025 (9,197 in 2024), that more than four in ten of these deaths and disappearances were on sea routes towards Europe, and that more than 82,000 migrants had died on these routes since 2014 <sup>[N105](#n105)</sup>. Citing UNHCR, Prothom Alo reported in February 2024 that 25 lakh people crossed the sea to Europe this way from 2015 to June 2023 and about 22,000 drowned, many Bangladeshis among them <sup>[N51 (2024)](#n51)</sup>. A Prothom Alo column of November 2025 put deaths and disappearances on the route at about 30,000 in 2014 to 2024 and, citing IOM figures reported in May 2024, said at least 283 Bangladeshis drowned trying to reach Europe via Libya from 2014 to early 2024 <sup>[N108](#n108)</sup>.
- **Returns:** at least 10,000 Bangladeshis have been flown home from Libya with IOM's help since 2017, including 175 on 26 February 2026 <sup>[N16](#n16), [N30](#n30)</sup>. Other charter flights home, mostly of people the Foreign Ministry says entered Libya illegally through traffickers to reach Europe by sea <sup>[N109 (2024)](#n109)</sup>:
  - 10 October 2024: more than 150 <sup>[N109 (2024)](#n109)</sup>;
  - 22 October 2024: 157 who asked to return from Misrata and nearby <sup>[N110 (2024)](#n110)</sup>;
  - 24 October 2025: 309 <sup>[N111](#n111)</sup>;
  - 18 November 2025: 170 <sup>[N112](#n112)</sup>;
  - 1 September 2026: 174 held in the Tajoura detention centre <sup>[N113](#n113)</sup>;
  - 27 October 2026: 174, of whom 36 had been held in a detention camp in Tripoli after being caught at sea and 41 were ill, with 175 more due from Misrata the next day <sup>[N114](#n114)</sup>.
- **Caught at sea:** IOM's annual report says the Libyan coast guard caught 21,700 irregular migrants in the Mediterranean in 2024, including 1,500 women and 700 minors <sup>[N115](#n115)</sup>.
- **Held in Libya:** in February 2025 the ambassador to Libya, Major General Abul Hasnat Muhammad Khairul Bashar, said 410 Bangladeshis caught crossing to Italy were in Libyan prisons, and that more than 21,000 Bangladeshis were working in Libya, 80% of them without valid papers <sup>[N115](#n115)</sup>. In 2023 the Embassy in Tripoli could not get Libyan exit permits to send home about 2,000 people <sup>[N10](#n10)</sup>. According to Bangladesh embassy sources in Tripoli cited in a Prothom Alo editorial of 6 February 2025, 4,200 Bangladeshis had been freed from various detention centres in Libya and sent home in the previous year and a half, and more than a thousand irregular Bangladeshis were still held in Libya <sup>[N46](#n46)</sup>. A Prothom Alo editorial of 25 February 2024 stated that BRAC's Migration Programme rescued and brought home 629 people in 2022 who had been living abroad without valid papers, and that they had been taken to Libya with the promise of attractive jobs in Europe <sup>[N73 (2024)](#n73)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How many Bangladeshis reached Italy by sea in 2025: 20,164, the 20,259 BRAC gives from Frontex for all of Europe, or the 17,000 sent to Italy by illegal routes "in the past year", from CID information cited in a Prothom Alo editorial of 1 April 2026?
> - How many Bangladeshis reached Italy by sea in January to June 2026: 4,249 (BRAC, from Frontex) or 3,691 (Prothom Alo, 5 September 2026)?
> - How many Bangladeshis die on the Mediterranean in a typical year: the "at least 100" or the "at least 500" two Prothom Alo editorials give, against IOM's count of at least 283 drowned in 2014 to early 2024, and from what source?

<a id="the-law"></a>

### 2.11 The law

- **Bangladesh:** under the 2026 Act, organising this journey is migrant smuggling, holding people for ransom is a form of exploitation under the trafficking offence, and the Act also covers offences committed abroad against Bangladeshis (see [The law in Bangladesh](#the-law-in-bangladesh)) <sup>[3](#o3)</sup>. The Minister for Expatriates' Welfare said on 18 April 2026 that the government had declared zero tolerance of the "mafia syndicate" at home and abroad and was determined to identify the Bangladeshi members of trafficking rings in Libya and elsewhere, bring them back and try them <sup>[N48](#n48)</sup>; four days earlier, on 14 April, he had said that strict action would be taken against "adam" traders (people-sending brokers) who send people abroad illegally, and that an inquiry into them had been ordered <sup>[N116](#n116)</sup>. The ambassador to Libya said in February 2025 that the government has a list of traffickers and dalals <sup>[N115](#n115)</sup>. A Prothom Alo editorial of 6 February 2025 stated that some licensed manpower-exporting firms are behind these broker rings and send people abroad through them, and that cases against many such firms in the past led to no action because many of those involved were influential people linked to the then ruling party <sup>[N46](#n46)</sup>.
- **Libya:** in April 2026 a Libyan court jailed four traffickers for up to 22 years <sup>[N117](#n117)</sup>.
- **Italy:** entering or staying without permission is an offence fined EUR 5,000 to 10,000 (about Tk 6.98 to 13.96 lakh) <sup>[13](#o13), [7](#o7)</sup>. If removal can't happen at once, the Questore (head of the provincial immigration police) can hold the person in a CPR, a detention centre before removal, for three months <sup>[14](#o14)</sup>. Expulsion brings a 3 to 5-year ban entered in the Schengen Information System, which bars every EU and Schengen country <sup>[15](#o15)</sup>. An Italian court did recognise one Bangladeshi kidnapped and tortured in Libya until his family paid as a trafficking victim <sup>[N118](#n118)</sup>.

**Cases against brokers in Bangladesh:**

- **March 2024, Faridpur.** A local Swechchhasebak League leader of Majhardia, Saltha, said Turap Molla, a people-sending broker, was the main accused in the case over Nur Alam of Majhardia, taken to Libya on the promise of Italy and tortured and killed on 12 February 2023, and that a court had issued a warrant for his arrest <sup>[N119 (2024)](#n119)</sup>. Saltha police arrested Turap outside the village mosque on 1 March 2024 and he got away: villagers said his relatives attacked the police and snatched him, and the police said his family pushed him and he ran <sup>[N119 (2024)](#n119)</sup>.
- **May 2024, Madaripur.** Sajal Bairagi's brother said the broker was telling the family not to file a case and had said he would return some money but was not giving it, and that, being poor and unable to afford a case, the family was trying to settle with the broker <sup>[N83 (2024)](#n83)</sup>. The Madaripur Superintendent of Police, Masud Alam, said police would give all legal help if relatives of the eight dead filed a case, but that the victims had little interest in filing cases <sup>[N83 (2024)](#n83)</sup>.
- **April 2024, Joypurhat and Lalmonirhat.** Afzal Hossain, father of Shamim Hossain, who was tortured in Libya, filed a case under the trafficking Act at Akkelpur police station on 1 April 2024 against the broker Mizanur Rahman of Temaria, his wife Rozina and his younger brother Shahinur; police had arrested none of them by the next day, and the father said Mizanur had taken 10 to 12 people with talk of good pay and hours, all of whom were tortured <sup>[N120 (2024)](#n120)</sup>. In Lalmonirhat, the mother of a kidnapped mason alleged that a different Libya-based Mizanur Rahman, through whom her son went, was behind the kidnapping; his father denied it, calling his son honest and hard-working, and Lalmonirhat Sadar police said they had received two written complaints <sup>[N21 (2024)](#n21)</sup>.
- **June 2024, Chattogram.** After returning, Azmir Hossain filed a case in a Chattogram court against six people, including the Libya-based broker and his brother; his lawyer said it was a criminal case over cheating and torture in Libya and that the court took cognisance of the complaint and ordered an investigation <sup>[N40 (2024)](#n40)</sup>.
- **July 2024, Bhairab.** On 5 July 2024, when the broker Rasel Mia came to his home in Jagannathpur, Bhairab, victims' families surrounded and beat him and handed him to police, who sent him to Kishoreganj district jail under the trafficking Act; the officer-in-charge of Bhairab police station said the father of one victim filed a case after the arrest and Rasel was sent to jail in that case <sup>[N56 (2024)](#n56)</sup>. Rasel Mia admitted under police questioning that he took money on the promise of sending people to Italy, and said he had made all the arrangements but could not send everyone because new problems had arisen on the sea route, at which everyone grew impatient <sup>[N56 (2024)](#n56)</sup>.
- **September 2024, Nasirnagar.** The Nasirnagar Upazila Nirbahi Officer (the government's chief administrator of the sub-district) said the administration had not known of the 26 men's cases until journalists told them, that none of the victims had complained to it, and that legal action would follow an investigation if a complaint came; relatives had held a human chain demanding the brokers' trial and compensation <sup>[N58 (2024)](#n58)</sup>. One man the families accuse, a cousin of the alleged ring leader, told Prothom Alo he had himself gone to Libya through that cousin, spent a month and a half in jail there with the others and 18 months in Libya in all, and took money from no one <sup>[N58 (2024)](#n58)</sup>.
- **2024 to January 2025, Mathbaria.** The wife of one Mathbaria hostage filed a case under the trafficking Act at Mathbaria police station in 2024; the station's officer-in-charge said in January 2025 that the investigation had been given to the district detective branch and that it could say what progress there was <sup>[N54](#n54)</sup>.
- **December 2024, Sylhet.** The father of the Jagannathpur man sent to Libya instead of Spain (see [The contract price and the final price](#the-contract-price-and-the-final-price)) filed a complaint with Sylhet's Human Trafficking Offence Suppression Tribunal on 27 November 2024, recorded as a case at Kotwali police station on 4 December; he said he had paid about BDT 25 lakh in all and had not spoken to his son for three and a half months <sup>[N121 (2024)](#n121)</sup>. At a press conference on 11 December a nephew of the chief accused called the case false, alleging the complainant's own son had lived in Libya since 2015 and that father and son took local young men to Libya in the name of Europe and held them for money <sup>[N121 (2024)](#n121)</sup>.
- **February 2025, Rajoir.** After the January 2025 sinking, local brokers in Rajoir went into hiding for fear of arrest; Madaripur's Additional Superintendent of Police said police were investigating the broker ring, and the Additional Deputy Commissioner said the district administration had started work through the embassy to bring the bodies home <sup>[N38](#n38)</sup>.
- **February 2025, Shariatpur.** Relatives of the 24 missing men filed 11 cases against Rashed Khan and his associates in the Shariatpur court and at Palong Model police station; the CID arrested him in Jatrabari, Dhaka, on 14 February 2025 in a case under the trafficking Act filed by the father of a missing man from Kalkini, Madaripur, and a Shariatpur magistrate sent him to jail the next day, fixing a later date to hear the police's remand request and his bail application <sup>[N39](#n39)</sup>. On 15 February, on hearing of the arrest, relatives of the missing men began a hunger strike in the grounds of the Shariatpur District and Sessions Judge's court demanding exemplary punishment and the tracing of the missing men <sup>[N39](#n39)</sup>.
- **February 2025, Dhaka.** RAB arrested Lipon Matubbar (35) of Rajoir and Anwar alias Ano Matubbar (40) of Bhanga, Faridpur, in Dhaka in cases under the trafficking Act filed at Bhanga and Rajoir police stations after relatives saw photos of the bodies of men who had set out for Italy on social media <sup>[N122](#n122)</sup>.
- **February 2025, Nasirnagar.** After Md Rasel Mia's death the broker from his village fled with his family; the officer-in-charge of Nasirnagar police station said legal steps would be taken if the victim's family gave a written complaint <sup>[N57](#n57)</sup>.
- **March 2025, Dhaka airport.** Police detained Md Fakhruddin, whom they described as the head of a Libya trafficking ring long accused of trafficking Bangladeshis to Libya, holding and torturing them and collecting ransom, when he landed from Cairo on 16 March 2025, along with a Biman Bangladesh Airlines traffic helper, Shawkat Ali, for allegedly helping him escape <sup>[N123](#n123)</sup>.
- **August 2025, Dhaka airport.** The CID arrested Sakhawat Hossain (42) of Madhuai, Feni Sadar, on his return from Libya on 21 August 2025; it said he had long helped run a mafia syndicate in Libya and said under questioning that he had sent about 400 to 500 Bangladeshis illegally to Italy on four boats <sup>[N98](#n98)</sup>.
- **July 2025, Kushtia.** Police arrested Robijul Islam (see [Libya to Italy by boat](#libya-to-italy-by-boat)) at Khoksa on 20 July 2025; the officer-in-charge of Islami University police station said there were at least six trafficking cases against him <sup>[N36](#n36)</sup>.
- **January 2026, Sylhet.** Sumon Mia's brother filed a case at Jalalabad police station, Sylhet, on 22 December 2025, and RAB arrested Sadek Ahmed (30) of Khashir village, Beanibazar, on 19 January 2026 <sup>[N80](#n80)</sup>.
- **March 2026, Sunamganj.** After the deaths of Sunamganj men on the way from Libya to Greece (12, according to this Prothom Alo report of 31 March 2026; Prothom Alo's report of 5 April 2026 under Deaths gives 13), cases were filed under the trafficking law against 9 dalals on the night of 30 March 2026, five at Jagannathpur police station and four at Dirai police station <sup>[N124](#n124), [N125](#n125)</sup>.
- **August 2026, Narail.** A case was filed against 34 people, including the alleged leaders Alauddin and Awal, over a Narail ring that held people in Libya for ransom <sup>[N76](#n76), [N75](#n75)</sup>.
- **October 2026, Faridpur.** Asked about the returnees' demand for trial of the Faridpur ring, the Additional Superintendent of Police (Crime and Ops) of Faridpur said on 14 October 2026 that legal action would be taken if the victims' families complained, and that steps would follow investigation if anyone had filed a case; one victim at the human chain asked, "Where do I go to get justice?" <sup>[N63](#n63)</sup>.
